"Allah has inspired in us the design to extinguish this hatred between our empires, to calm these revolutions that spread calamities upon men, to make known to those who have given us this counsel the path in which Allah has directed us, which consists in applying all our solicitude to pursue this design worthy of all hopes, to see humanity healed of the sufferings that overwhelm it, and to adjourn indefinitely the means to which it is appropriate to resort only as a supreme remedy. "For we are not at all inclined to brandish the iron of our sword for combat, before having clearly shown the right path that it is appropriate to follow; we will not permit an appeal to the fate of arms until after having established our right in a brilliant way, after having gathered all the proofs in our favor. "What has confirmed our design to realize our projects in all that
concerns the means to improve the lot of the Muslims, to have executed what has appeared to us susceptible of causing the prosperity of the peoples, were the admonitions of the Shaikh al-Islam, the model of men who have attained metaphysical knowledge, Kamal ad-Din 'Abd ar-Rahman, who


[p. 504] {#po.14.p504}
الاسلام قدوة العارفين كمال الدين عبد الرحمن الى هو نعم العون لنا فى امورنا واشار بذلك! رحمة من الله لمن دعلا ونقمة على من اعرض عنه وعصاه© فانفذنا اقضى القضاة قطب الدين ولاتابك باء الدين الذين هما من ثمات" هذه الدولة الزاهرة والمملكة الفاهرة ليعزفاهم طريقتنا وتحّتى عندهم ما تنطوى عليه لسنوم اللسلمين جيل نيتنا وتنا لبم اننا من الله على بصيرة وان الاسلام يجتب ما قبله وان تعالى القى فى رونا ان نتتع الح واهله» وشاهدون عظيم نعمة الله على الكاقة بما دعانا اليه
has favored us with his aid in the affairs of our State, and who has advised us to act in this way; may the mercy of Allah be upon him who invokes Him, may His vengeance fall upon him who disobeys His precepts, who rebels against His orders! "We (have) sent you the most perspicacious of judges, Kotb ad-Din, and the atabek Baha ad-Din, who are both personages worthy of all trust of this resplendent State, of this victorious empire, so that they may make known to you the path in which we have the intention to walk, in such a way that you may be well convinced of all that implies the excellence of the projects that we have formed for the community of the Muslims. We have explained to you that it is Allah who has gratified us with the intelligence (that removes the veils of ignorance), that Islam destroys all that existed before it, that Allah, the Most High, has made the desire to follow without truce, nor respite, the path of truth penetrate our spirit, and
to take our example from those who practice His worship. "You are a witness to the immensity of the benefits that Allah spreads over the totality of His people, when He has invited us to concern ourselves with making


[p. 505] {#po.14.p505}
من تقديم اسباب الاحسان فلا يحرموها! بالنظر الى سالف الاحوال! فكل يوم هو فى شأن فان تطلعت نفوسهم الى دليل تستحكم بسبه دواعى الاعتماد وحيّة يتقون" ها من بلوغ المراد فلينظروا الى ما ظهر من ما لمرنادة مما اشتهن خيرلا وعم ائره» فانّا ابتدأنا بتوفيق الله تعالى بإعلاء اعلام الدين واظهاره فى ايراد كل امر واصداره تقديما واقامة نواميس الشرع المحتدى على "مقتضى قانون العدل الاحمدى اجلالاً وتعظيما وادخانا السرور على قلوب الجمبور وعفونا عن كل من اجترح سّئة4 و اقترى ول قابلتلا الصفح وقلنا عفا الله عمّا سلف© وتنقتمنا باصلاح امور اوقاف المسلمين من المساجد
live the works that contribute to the prosperity of men; do not turn your gaze from this spectacle to carry it toward the events of the past, for each day finds itself characterized by a situation that is proper to it. If your spirit seeks an argument that establishes by itself, in an unshakeable way, all the reasons to have confidence in our affirmations, a proof that leads you to absolute certainty, as much as you can conceive the desire for it, consider what has resulted from the orders that we have given, what is proclaimed by the public voice, the results of which are visible in all the world. "Indeed, by the grace of Allah, the Most High, we have begun to raise the flags of the faith, to manifest it in the promulgation of every command, to concern ourselves with it before any other affair, to put into practice the precepts of the Muhammadan law, following the requirements of the code of Ahmadian justice to glorify and adore Allah! We have made joy penetrate the hearts; we have granted our pardon to all those who have committed crimes, and who have rendered themselves guilty of reprehensible acts. We have treated the sinner with indulgence, and we have said to him: "May Allah forgive you the faults of your past." "We have commanded to put in order the affairs that concern


[p. 506] {#po.14.p506}
والمشاهد والمدارس وعمارة يماع البق والربط الدوارس وايصال حاصلها بموجب ! عوائدها القديمة على القاعدة المستقيمة لمستحتهاة بشروط4 واقفباة بعد اصلاح ما دش من تألفهاة ومنعنا ان يلتمس شىء متا استحدث عليها وان لا يغيّر شىء7 مما قزر اقلا فيها واسند اليهاة© وامرنا بتعظيم امر الحاج9 وتجبيز وفدها وتأمين سيلبا وتسيير قوافلبا واطلقنا سبيل التجار !1 الّذين هم عمارة الامصار وكذلك!! المتردّدين الى تلك البلاد ليسافروا بحسب *! اختيارهماعلى احسن قواعدهم تطمينا للعباد امنين على أنفسهم من
the religious foundations of the Muslims, such as mosques, funerary monuments, colleges; to repair the edifices in which the piety of men is manifested, the convents in ruins; to make their income reach, following the ancient customs, consecrated by constant usage, the persons who have the qualification to dispose of them, according to the conventions that have been stipulated by the authors of the pious foundations, after having re-established in their integrity the provisions that had been annulled. We have forbidden the collection of any sum whatsoever of the taxes that have been newly imposed on these foundations; we have forbidden that one change anything whatsoever to the primitive provisions that have been established with regard to them, and to the attributions that have been made to them. "We have ordered that one treat with all regard the affairs of the pilgrims, that one take care to organize their departure, to ensure the security of the roads they must follow, and the march of the caravans; we have rendered all freedom of transit to the merchants, on whom rests the prosperity
of the empires; we have acted in the same way with regard to the travelers who go to your empire, so that they may travel according to their good pleasure, to the best of their convenience; so that men


[p. 507] {#po.14.p507}
حوادث الفساد! وحوينا على السساكى والقراول! والشحانى فى الاطراف التعرض ليم فى مصادرهم ومواردهم وان يمشون حيث شاؤوا على احسن ما كانت عادتهم من قواعدهم» وقد كان صادف قراول. لنا3 حاسوسا فى زتى الفتقير كان سبيل مثله ان يهلك4 اذ سعا" الى حتفه قدمه4 ولم يهرق دمه بحرمة9 ما حومه7 الله تعالى وأعدتاا الهم ولا يطفى عنهم ما كان فى انفاذ الجواسيس من الضرر العام للمسلمين© فانٌ عساكرنا طال ما رأوهم فى زتى الفقرة وناك واهل الصلاح فساءت ظنونهم حتّى قتلوا من قتلوا من
enjoy tranquility, that travelers be reassured about their lot, and do not fear the attacks of crime; we have formally forbidden the soldiers and the men who watch the roads as well as the police officers who are in the frontier provinces of our empire, to disturb them, either when they leave our States, or when they return to them, in such a way that they may go where it suits them, to the best of the habits they have taken, according to their convenience. "One of our soldiers charged with the surveillance of the roads had captured a spy dressed as a fakir; the treatment applied to his likes consisted of the death penalty, for he had rushed himself toward death; but his blood has not been shed, out of respect for the prohibitions that have been promulgated by Allah, the Most High. We have returned this individual to you. You are not unaware of what universal damage the sending of spies causes to the Muslims. "Indeed, for a long time already, our soldiers, seeing the spies dressed in the costume of fakirs, hermits, religious, have conceived malicious intentions against these people, so much so that, on several occasions, they have killed
persons who belonged to these categories, without them having committed the least crime, or the least offense. Thanks be to Allah for it! the need to resort to such subterfuges has disappeared since the roads and the paths have become safe, since the merchants and the other travelers have been able to circulate at their will.


[p. 508] {#po.14.p508}
هذه الطوائف بغير حريمة ولا حناح ! فانّ ارتفعت الحاحة بحمد الله تعالى الى ذلك بما تأمنت الطرق والمسالك3 وترّد التجار وغيرهم4© فاذا يمعنوا من الفكر فى هذا الامور واطشنان الجمبور لا يخفى عنهم انها اخلاق حيليّة طبيعّة وعن -شوائب التكلف ولتصنّع عريّة© واذا كانت الحال على ذلك فقد ارتفعت دواعى المضئةٍ الى كانت توجب المخالفة4 فاته كانت بطريق الدين الب عن حوزة المسلمين فقد ظهر بفضل الله تالى: فى دولتنا النور" المبين وان كانت لما سبق من الاسباب فمن7 يجرى الآن طريق الصواب فانّ له عندنا الزلفى وحسن مآبب© وقد رفنا الحجاب واتينا يفضل الخطاب" وعرفناهم
"If you consider attentively in your spirit these results, and the tranquility that the peoples enjoy, you will perceive without difficulty that we have been guided by innate and essential qualities, that we have not used a dishonorable affectation of sentiments that would not be our own, and that we have in no way tried to deceive you by the display of false sentiments. "Since things have arrived at this stage, all the reasons that led us to harm one another, and that were the source of the hostility that reigned between us, have disappeared; for this hostility was produced by the difference of our religions, and by the obligation in which you found yourself to defend the Muslim empire. Now, by divine grace, the light of truth has manifested itself in our States, and, if there have been, in times past, motives for hostility between us, he who, today, follows the right path, is certain to find with us a favorable welcome, and an asylum worthy of his merit. "We have lifted the veils (of enmity that separated us), and we have arrived at the grace of engaging in a conversation with you. We have


[p. 509] {#po.14.p509}
ما غزمنا عليه بنّة خالصة لله تالى لنعلم ما عندهم من الجواب! وحزينا على جبيع عساكرنا العمل بخلافها لنرضى با الله والرسول وتلوح على صفحاتها آثار الاقبال والقبول وتستريح من اختلاف الكلمة هذه الاتمة وتتجلى بنور الايتلاف ظلمة الاختلاف والغمّةه» فيسكن فى سابع ظلها البوادى والحواض وتنقى القلوب التى بلغت من الجهد الاجر وسفى عن سالف9 البنات والجرائر3 ونريح المسلمين من فكر يفتّت المرائر فان وف الله سلطان مصر لاختبار ما فيه صلاح .العالم وانتظام امور بنى ادم فقد وجب عليه التمتك بلعروة الوتقى4 وسلوك الطريقة المثلى بفتح ابواب الطاعة والاتحاد وبل
made our projects known to you, in a pure intention to satisfy the desires of Allah, the Most High, to know the response that you have the design to make to us. We have expressly forbidden all our soldiers to act in a way contrary to our wishes, so as to satisfy, by this conduct, Allah and the Prophet, so that the dawn of happiness and felicity may rise over the world, so that, henceforth, the Muslim community may be sheltered from all divergence, so that the shadows of contradiction and pain may be dissipated by the light of union. "Then, the nomadic tribes, as well as the men who dwell in the cities, will live in peace, in the breadth of the shadow of our protection, and the hearts will find tranquility again, after having almost broken in the breasts of men; the sins and the crimes that they have committed will be forgiven them, and we will thus deliver the Muslims from the worries that the bitterness of their condition imposed upon them. "If Allah grants the sultan of Egypt the favor of choosing what can ensure the salvation of the world, and of ordering in a happy way the condition of humanity, it is his strict obligation, to attain this goal, to put
into action the firmest measures, to follow the most perfect path, to open the doors of submission to divine orders and of concord, to lavish the efforts of his virtue so that prosperity may be returned to these kingdoms and to these provinces, so that these violent troubles may be appeased, so that


[p. 510] {#po.14.p510}
الاخلاص بحيث تنعمر تلك! الممالك والبلاد وتسكن .الفتنة التائرةة وتغمد السيوف البائرة4 وتحل الكاقّة ارض البونا وروض البدون وتخلص رقاب المسلمين من اغلال الذلٌ والبون© وان غلب سوء الظنّ بما تفضّل به واهب الرحمة ومنع من معرفة9؟ قدر هد النعمة فقد شكر الله مساعيناة وابلى عذرنا مقبولا؟ وما كنا معذبين احتى ثبث رسولا واللّه الموقّق للرشاد والسداد وهو الممنّن9 على البلاد والععاد وحسنا الله وحده» كتب فى اواسط حمادى الال سنة احدى وثمانين وستماثة للببجرة0! بمقام ألاطاق 0!»
may the sharp-edged swords return to their scabbards, so that men may inhabit the land of serenity and the groves of tranquility, so that the neck of the Muslims may be delivered from the yoke of shame and humiliation. "If your distrust, regarding the graces granted to us by Him who is the dispenser of all mercy, triumphs (over the arguments we have given you), if this distrust prevents you from recognizing the value of these divine favors, it is enough for us that Allah has rewarded our efforts, and that He has manifested that He accepted our excuse, for 'We have never punished (a people) without sending them a prophet.' Certainly, Allah is the One who enables men to walk in the path of salvation and to reach the truth; He is the One who spreads His benefits upon
جواب السلطان الملك البنصور بانشاء !1 محتى“! الدين ابن عند الظاهر»
the world and upon His servants. The protection of the one God is sufficient for us (to guarantee us against the vicissitudes of fortune). "This was written in the middle of the month of Jumada I of the year 681, in the encampment of Alatagh."


[p. 511] {#po.14.p511}

### Reply of the Sultan al-Malik al-Mansour by the pen of Mohyi ad-Din Ibn Abd az-Zahir
بسم الله الرحمن الرحيم بق الله تعالى باقبال دولة السلطان الملك المنصور» كلام قلاوون الى السلطان احمد© أما بعد حمد لله الذى اوضح با ولنا الحقّ! منهاجً وجاء بنا فجاء نصر الله والفتع ودخل اناس فى الدين افواحً والصلاة على سيّدنا ونيناك محتّد آله« وقد وصل الكتاب الكريم المتلقّاة بالتكريم المشتمل على الناء العظيم من دخوله فى الدين وخروجه عثن خلّف من العشيرة والاقريين ولمّا فتح هذا الكتاب فائح هذا الخ المعلّم المعظم9 والحديث الى صتّح7 عند اهل8 الاسلام اسلامه واصحّ الحديث
"In the name of Allah, the Clement and Merciful! By the power of Allah, the Most High! By the fortune of the reign of Sultan al-Malik al-Mansour. "Word of Qalawun to Sultan Ahmad! Therefore, praise be to Allah who has manifested, for us and through us, the path that leads to the truth; it is He who has guided our steps, during which we have been supported by His aid and accompanied by victory, such that men have entered the Religion in crowds. May peace be upon our lord and prophet Muhammad and upon his family! "The august letter that you wrote to us has reached us; we have received, with all the respect it deserves, this missive, which contains the capital news you give us of your conversion to the (Muslim) faith, of your renunciation of the tradition of the men of your nation and the members of your family. When this letter was opened (we saw that) it began with the announcement of this event, which teaches us a fact of exceptional importance, and with the narration of these vicissitudes, which are, for the faithful of Islam, the tangible proof of the validity of your Islam, for the most authentic account is what is given as said by a Muslim.


[p. 512] {#po.14.p512}
ما روى عن مسلم© وتوبّبت الوحوا بالدعاء الى الله سحانه فى ان يتبّته! على ذلك بالقول التابت وان .بنت حب حب هذا الدين فى قله كما انته احسن النبت من احسن المنابت© وحصل الأمقل للفصل المبتدأك بذكره من حديث اخلاصه النيّة5 فى اول العسس وعنفوان الصاء الى الاقرار بالوحدانيّة ودخوله فى الملة المحتديّة بالقول والعمل والنتّة فالحمد لله على ان شرح صدر للاسلام والبمه شيف هذا الالبام كحمدنا لله على ان حعلنا من السابقين الاقلين الى هذا الدين والى هذا المقال والمقام ونتّت9 اقدامنا فى
"Our faces have turned in prayer toward Allah, may He be blessed! so that He may make your resolution unshakable on this point by the irrefutable word [of the Koran], so that He may cause to grow in your heart the seed of the love of this religion, just as He has (already) caused it to germinate, through the most wonderful vegetation, in the most excellent soil. "Our reflection has been exercised on the initial paragraph of your letter, in which you mention, regarding the purity of your faith, the firm intention in which you have held yourself, from your childhood and the flower of your youth, to recognize the unity of Allah, to enter into the religion of Muhammad, in word, in deed, and in thought. Praise be rendered to Allah for having opened your heart to Islam, and for having inspired you with this noble design; praise be also testified to Him for having made us your precursors and your elders in this religion, in this confession, in this stage, for having made our steps firm in all the posts
where there was fighting to be done, where it was necessary to struggle for the testimony of our faith, and for having granted us this blessing without which the walk of man stumbles and goes astray.


[p. 513] {#po.14.p513}
كل موقف احتباد وجهاد1 تتزلزل دونه الاقدام© واما افضاء النوة فى الملك وميراثه بعد والده واخيه الكبير اليه وافاضة8 جلابيب هذه النعمة4 عليه وتوقله الاسوة التى طهّرها ايمانه واظهرهاة سلطانه فلقد اورنها الله من اصطفلا من عياده وصدق المبشرات له من كرامة اللي الله وعباده© وأا حكاية اجتماع الاخوان والاولاد والامرآ الكبار ومقدّمى العساكر وزعما البلاد فى مجع قورلتاى ؟ الذى تنقدح فيه زبدة الار77 وان كلمتهم انفقت على ما سيقت به كلمة اخيه الكبير فى انفاذ العساكر الى هذا الجانب وان نكر فيما اجتمعت عليه ارآؤهم وانتبت اليه اهواؤهم فوجده مخالفاً لما فى ضميرك اذ قصده الصلاح ورأيه الاصلاح إنه
"As for your accession to the empire, the fact that you inherited it after your father and your elder brother, that Allah has granted you the adornment of this grace, that you have ascended the throne which your faith purifies and to which your royalty will give a new splendor, Allah causes to inherit sovereign power whomsoever of His servants He chooses for this purpose, and He realizes the promises He has made to him through the miraculous power of His saints and those who serve Him in obedience. "You then speak to us of the gathering of your brothers the princes, the great emirs, the commanders of the armies, the governors of the provinces of your empire, in the diet of the kurultai, in which the best design is manifested; you tell us that the unanimity of the opinions of all these personages turned toward the execution of the project that your elder brother had conceived at an earlier time, to march troops against our empire; but, that after having reflected on this program, which represents the unanimity of the judgments of the persons who attended this diet, and the definitive result of their opinions, you found that it was in absolute contradiction
with the designs that you nourish in your mind, since your goal is the universal good, your intention, to give peace to men, that then you extinguished the flames of this fire, and that you calmed the tumult of these angers.


[p. 514] {#po.14.p514}
اطفاً تلك الائرة وسكن تلك اثائرة تهذا فمل الملك السّقى المشفق من قومه على من بقى المفكّر فى العواقب بالرأى التاقب! وألًا فلو تركوا اداتهم حتى تحمليم الغو لكات تكون هذ الكرة هى الكوة لكن هو تمن خاف مقام ربّه ونبى النفس عن البوى ولم يوافق قول من ضلَ ولا فمل من غوى © ولا القول منه4 انه الا بحب المشارعة الى المقارعة الا بعد ايضاح الححية وتركيب الحجة54 فاتظامه فى سلك الايمان صارت حيّتنا وحجّته المتركبة على من عدت طواغيته؟ عن سلوك هذد المحة متنكية؟
"This conduct is that of the pious sovereign who watches with solicitude over what remains of his people, who reflects on what events have in store for him, with a perspicacious mind. Otherwise, if these people had let themselves go to accomplish their designs, if they had let themselves be deceived by their illusions, this expedition would have been the ruin of your empire. But you have acted like one who fears the majesty of his Lord, who turns his soul away from passions whose speech does not accord at all with that of men who wander like the lost, nor the acts with the actions of people who are blinded. "As for your assertion that you do not like to hasten to appeal to the fate of arms, before having shown, in an evident way, the path of truth that it is fitting to follow, before having established your right in an irrefutable manner, your entry into the road of the true faith is for us the proof, and a bundle of proofs, that you can invoke against those who have let themselves be diverted from the right path by the priests of infidelity in your empire and who wander at random outside the path of truth. Certainly Allah, may He be glorified! and all men, know


[p. 515] {#po.14.p515}
فانّ الله سبحانه والناس كاقّة قد علموا ان قيامنا انما هو لنصرة هذ الملّة وجهادنا واجتهادنا انما هو على الحقيقة لله وحيت! قد دخل معنا فى الدين هذا الدخول فقد ذهبت الاحقاد وزالت الحول؟ وارتفاع المنافرة تحصل المظافرةة فالاييان كالبنيان شد بعضه بعض ومن اتام مناره فله اهل بأهل فى كُل مكان وجيران بجران فى كُل ااض© فانا ترتيب هذه القواعد الحميدةة على اذكار شيخ الاسلام تقدوة العارفين كمال الدين عبد الرحمن اعاد الله من بركانه7 قد اشار فانه نعم المستشارة8 فلم .يرأ لولتى قبله8 كرامة كهذه الكرامة
that we have never acted except with a view to the triumph of this religion, that the wars we have sustained, that the efforts to which we have devoted ourselves, have had no other object than to attain the truth in Allah. "And, since you have thus entered with us into the Religion, the hatreds have vanished, the hostilities have disappeared; the end of the dissensions that separated us will provoke an intimate collaboration, for the Faith is like an edifice whose stones all supported one another. He who has taken the Faith as the lighthouse (which must guide his path) will find a family in every family, in every country, friends among all men, in every land. "If the establishment of this happy situation is due to the admonitions of the Shaykh al-Islam, the model of men who have attained metaphysical knowledge, Kamal ad-Din 'Abd ar-Rahman, may Allah fill him with His blessings! for he has advised, and he who has consulted him has rejoiced to have followed his advice, and one has never seen before him that a saint has been gratified
with such an extraordinary miraculous power. We hope, thanks to his mystical power and the supernatural power of the saints, that the City of Peace (Baghdad-Dar al-Islam), like every house (dar), will be


[p. 516] {#po.14.p516}
والرجاء ببركته ونركة الصالحين ان1 تتح دار السلام وك دار للاسلام وهى دار اقامته! حتّى تتعّ شرائط الاببان وحود شمل الاسلام مجتمعا كأحسن مما كانه ولا ينكر لمن لكرامته ابتداء هذا التمكين4 فى الوجود انّ كل حقّ الى نصابه ببركته يعود8 وام انفاذ اقضىة القضاة قطب الملّة9 والدين والاتابك بياء الدين7 الموثوق فى تقلبما” فى ابلاغ رسائل هذا البلاغة فقد حضروا واعادوا كل قول حسن9 من حوالى احواله وخطرات خاطر ومنتظرات
opened to Islam, it which was its metropolis, so that the ritual prescriptions imposed by the faith may be accomplished in their integrity and that the Muslim world may take on a cohesion even more perfect than that which it enjoyed in the past. "It will not be an arduous task for him who, by the supernatural power (with which Allah has gratified him), has been the origin of this appeasement in the state (of our relations), to bring to fruition, by the grace that accompanies him, everything that is the expression of the truth. "Regarding the embassy of the most penetrating of judges, Qutb ad-Din, and of the atabeg Baha ad-Din, of the mission of trust with which they were invested to make us receive the letters all full of your eloquence, these two personages presented themselves before us, and they repeated to us a host of excellent words that relate to your situation, to the thoughts
that agitate your mind, to the projects that you carry within you; all that is worthy of praise and commendation, and the account that was given to us of it flows from the most excellent source.


[p. 517] {#po.14.p517}
ناظره؟ ومن كل ما يشكر وحمد وسنعن حديثها فيه عن مسند احمد© واما الاشارة الى ان النفوى ان كانت تمتطلع الى اقامة دليل تستحكم بيه دواعى الود الجبيل فاينظر الى ما ظهن من ماثر فى موارد الامر ومصادرا و1 من العدل والاخسان بالقلب واللسان والمتقدم باصلاح الاوقاف والمساجد والربط والمشاهد وتسهيل السبيل" للحج© نهذه صفات من يريد المملكة للدوام3 فلعًا ملك عدل ولم يمل4 الى لوم من عدى ولا لوم من عذل على انها وان كانت من الافعال الحسنة والمتوات5 التى تستنطق بالدعاء الالسنة فبى واحبات تؤقى وقريات بمثلبا يبدّى وهو اكبر من انه باجراء اجر غيرة؟ يتخ وعليه
"You tell us that, if they want to arrive at discovering the existence of a proof that establishes in an irrefutable way the reality of the perfect affection (that you feel for us), minds have only to consider the results that have been manifested by your acts, as much the events that have occurred, as the orders that you have given to provoke them, the justice and the benefits (that you have spread upon your subjects), as much by the sentiments that agitate your heart, as by your words, the commandments that you have promulgated for the re-establishment of pious foundations, of mosques, of convents, of funerary monuments, to facilitate for pilgrims the road toward the holy cities. "These are the qualities of the sovereign who wants his kingdom to last eternally, who spreads justice upon men when he has arrived at the throne, who does not feel himself attracted by the vileness of those who act in injustice, who does not feel the need to blame those who slander him. So much so that, if these are meritorious actions, works worthy of celestial rewards, that men celebrate in their prayers, these are strict obligations, which must be accomplished of all necessity, and pious works, such as those by which one begins on the path of truth; you are too great a prince to glorify yourself for having rendered to another what belonged to him to hold yourself to this work, and pretend to make merits of it for yourself.


[p. 518] {#po.14.p518}
يقتصرا :ا له يذكّرنه بل! انما تفتخر الملوك الاكابر بر ممالك8 على ملوكها ونظمها ما كات عليه فى سلوكها وقد كان والده فعل شيا من ذلك9 مع الملوك السلجوقتة وغيرهم وما كان أحد منهم بدينه يدين” ولا دخل معه فى ذين8 واقزهم9 فى ملكيم وما0! زحزخهم عن ملكهم8 وجب عليه انه لا يرى حقًا مغتصاً وأنى !! آلا رك ولا باع ممتة بالظلم وورضى الا صتد! حتّى انّ اسباب ملكه تقوى وايّامه تتزيّن بافعال التقوى
"For, on the contrary, the greatest kings have put their pride in rendering their kingdoms to their (legitimate) sovereigns, in restoring to them the organization on which they lived (before they had conquered them). It is in this sense that your father acted with regard to the Seljuk kings, and also other sovereigns; however, none of them had converted to his faith, none of them had entered into the same confession as him. Despite that, he confirmed them in the possession of their kingdoms, and he did not precipitate them down from their thrones. "It is your strict obligation, when you see a right openly violated, not to pass by without redressing this wrong; you must not see an arm extended for injustice, and tolerate that it not be turned away (from the object it wanted to reach), so that the bases on which is
founded your empire may be consolidated, so that the days of your reign may be adorned with the works of piety.


[p. 519] {#po.14.p519}
وامما تحريمه على الشحانى وعلى عساكر والقراولات فى الاطراف! الى التعّض الى احدٌ + بالاذى واصفاء موارد الواددين والصادرين من شوائب العدتى3 فمن حين بلفنا انهم تقتمواة بمثل ذلك تقتمنا ايضا بمثله الى سائر تابنا بالرحبة والبيرة وعين تاب والى مقتى الساكر5 باطراف تلك الممالك© واذا اتخد الايمان وانتقدت الايمان تحتّم هذا الاحكام؟ وترّب عليه جميع الاحكام8 واتا الجاسوس الفقير الذى امسك واطلق وان سبب من يثزبًا من الجواسيس بزى الفقراء قتل جماعة من الفقرآء الصلحاة رجما بالف هذا باب من تلقاء ذلك الجانب كان فتحه وزند من ذلك الطرف كان قدحه و من ابشرى
"As for what relates to the prohibition that you have made to the police officers, to your soldiers, to the guards of the roads, in the frontier provinces of your empire, to harm any person, from the fact that you have removed from the path of those who come, and of those who go, the obstacles that constituted the enemies (who were lying in wait to attack them), as soon as we learned that you had given orders in this sense, we sent our instructions in this same spirit to all our governors, at ar-Rahba, at al-Bira, at 'Ayn-tab, and to the commanders of the troops on the marches of our domains. "When one practices the same faith, when oaths have been exchanged, that is an invariable base, on which all decisions must be founded. "As for what is of the spy (disguised as) a fakir who was captured, then to whom one rendered liberty, of your affirmation following which, from the fact that spies have disguised themselves by taking the costume of fakirs, many pious fakirs have been killed, victims of the error (caused by this trickery), that is a door that, on your side, you were the first to
open, and a tinderbox from which you, the first, imagined drawing fire. How many of these individuals disguised as fakirs, and coming from your States, have you sent us, how many have you dispatched to us to spy on our affairs?


[p. 520] {#po.14.p520}
يفقيرً! من ذلك الجانب سرود والى الاطلاع على الامور ستزدود» واظفن للهلا منهم بجماعة كثيرة فرفع عنهم السيف وام يكشف ما غطود بخرقة الفقر بلم4 ولا كيف» واتا الاشارة الى ان بأنفاق الكلمة تنجلى ظلمة الاختلانى وتدرّةٌ بها من الخيرات9 الاخلاف ونكون بها صلاح العالم وانتظام شمل بنى ادم فلا را لمن فتح باب الاتحاد وجح للسلم" فقد جاد ولا حاد" ومن تمنى عنانه عن المكافحة كان كمن مد يد المصالحة للمصافحةة والصلم10 وان كان سيّد الاحكام ولا ب من أمور تبنى عليه ! قواعده وحم من مدلوله؟!
"Allah has permitted us to capture a considerable number of these individuals; one has turned away from their head the sword (that should have cut short their existence), and one has not sought to discover what they were concealing under the robe pierced with holes of poverty through interrogations and contradictory discussions. "Concerning what you tell us that the religious unity (which henceforth reigns between us) will dissipate the shadows of hostility, that it will be the source of the most diverse prosperities, that it will give salvation to the world, that, through it, the fate of humanity will be definitively settled, certainly, no obstacles will be opposed to him who has opened the door of union and who has manifested his tendencies for peace, for he has done a work of generosity, and he has not acted in error. He who has turned the bridle of his horse from the path of hostility is like him who has extended his hand to found the alliance and bring back peace. "Certainly peace is the most excellent of laws (that are imposed on humanity); it cannot absolutely do without facts on which its bases rest, and thanks to which one can know the proof of its utility. The facts that are articulated in your letter are generalities of strict obligation, on which all realities rest; they indicate quite simply if the state of peace is possible, or the opposite. But, in addition to these facts, there


[p. 521] {#po.14.p521}
فوائده فالامور المسطورة فى كتابه هى كلنات لازمة يعمس بها كل معنى ومعلم! ان تبأ صلح او لم وثع امور لا بد ان تعقد وتحكم؟ وفى سلكبا عقود العهود تنظم8 قد تحتلا بلسان المشافهة النى اذا اوردت3 اقنات ان شاء الله عليها النفوس واحرزتها صدوز الرسائل كأحسن ما تحرزه سطور الطروس© واتا الاشارة الى قوله7 تعالى وما كنا معذبين حتى تبعث رسولا قما على هذا النسق من الود ينسج ولا على هذا السبيل ينيج بل لفضل المتقتم" فى الدين ونصرلا عبود" ترعى وافادات تستدعى وما برح الفضل للأولوة9! وان
are other acts that must, of an evident necessity, be concluded, and be solidly studied, by the way of which treaties of alliance are contracted. "We have had the conditions of these treaties carried to you by word of mouth which, when they are exposed to you, will be found agreed to, if it pleases Allah, by the spirit (of those who will hear them), and which will be kept with care in the memory of the heads of our department of foreign affairs in a way as perfect as in the lines traced on paper. "As for what you want to say by citing the word of Allah, may He be exalted! 'We have never punished (a people) before having addressed a prophet to them', that is a fact that has no relation to the dispositions that must ensure our friendship, nor anything to do with what you expose to us. But, from the merit that belongs to him who, the first, entered into the Religion, and devoted himself to its defense, were born rights (that those who embraced it after him must respect), and prerogatives
of which he can legitimately avail himself. And this preeminence, which is the prerogative of our possession (of priority) is not prescribed, even when plurality comes to join the unique primordial. If you had considered


[p. 522] {#po.14.p522}
تنناهى العدد للواحد! الال ولو تأثل مورد هذا الاية فى غير مكانا لتروى وأرّل» وعند ما انتبينا الى جواب ما لعلّه يجب عنه الجواب من فصول الكتاب سمعنا المشافبة التى على لسان اقضى القضاة قطب الدين فكان مبنا ما يناسب3 ما فى هذا الكتاب من دخوله فى الدين وانتظام عقده بسلك المؤمنين وما بسطه من معدلة واحسان مشكورة4 بلسان كل انسان© فالمنّة لله على ذلك فلا نشبها منه بامتنان وقد انزل الله على رسوله فى حقّ من أمتنّ بإسلامه قل لا تمنّوا على اسلامكم بل الله يسّ عليكم أن هداك للايبان» ومن المشافهة أنّ الله قد اعطائا من العطاء ما اغناه عن امتداد الطرف الى ما فى يد غيل
the inconveniences that there were in citing this verse in a passage where it has nothing to do, certainly, that would have given you pause, and you would have acted with less precipitation. "As we were about to set ourselves to reply to certain paragraphs of your letter, which, perhaps, would have deserved that one replied to them, we heard the communications that you charged the most penetrating of judges, Qutb ad-Din, to make to us by word of mouth. That was an exposition that confirmed what is found explained in your letter on your conversion to the faith of Islam, on the fact that you came to take your place in the community of true believers, that you have spread upon your subjects the justice and the benefits that attract upon you the blessings of all men. "Praise be rendered to Allah for it, without one insisting more lengthily on the recognition that He owes you for it, for Allah revealed to His Envoy, regarding the man who pretends to make a merit of his Islamism to Him: 'Say to them! Do not pretend to oblige Me by your Islamism,
for it is on the contrary Allah who has filled you with His favors, by this fact that He has directed your steps in the road of the true faith!'" "Among the verbal communications (that he made to us on your behalf, the qadi Qutb ad-Din told us) that Allah has gratified you with gifts (so


[p. 523] {#po.14.p523}
من ارض وماء فان حصلت! الرغية فى الاتّفاق على ذلك! فالامر حاصل© فالجواب انّ مع امود" متى حصات عليها" الموافقة ابتنا على ذلك حكم المصاحية والمصادقة ورأى الله والناس كيف يكون تصافينا واذلالة عدونا واعزاز مصافينا كم من صاحب وحد حيت9 لا يوجد الاب والاخ والقرابة8 وما تم أمر” هذا الدين واستحكم فى صدورة الاسلام الا بمظافرة؟ الصحابة وان كانت له رغية مصروفة الى الاتحاد 0!وحسن الوداد وجبيل الاعتضاد !1 وكبت الاعداء والاضداد والاستناد الى من يشتدّ الامى به13 عند الاستناد فالرأى اليه فى ذلك© ومن المشافهة انه انّ كات الرغية ممتدتة الامل الى ما فى بده من ارض وماء
extraordinary) that they permit you not to cast looks of envy on the possessions of others, on land or on sea, and that, consequently, if we have the desire to conclude an agreement on this base, that is a settled affair. "We will answer you on this subject, that when one agrees on these premises, it is on them that the order of friendship and sincerity is founded. Allah and men will see of what nature will be the sincerity of the relations that we will maintain, how we will humiliate our enemies, in what way we will glorify our friends. How many allies does one not find, while one finds (to defend oneself) neither father, nor brother, nor relatives! "The order of this religion could not arrive at its perfection, it did not implant itself in an unrootable way in the heart of the faithful of Islam except by the mutual assistance that the companions of Muhammad lent one another. If you are truly animated by a lively desire to arrive at absolute unity with us, to maintain with our person relations imprinted with a perfect friendship, to second us as best as it will be in your power, to reduce to impotence our enemies and our adversaries, to lean on who possesses the necessary force to support you, you are the master to act thus. "You have had it said to us that your ambitions extend only to the desire


[p. 524] {#po.14.p524}
فلا حاجة الى انفاذ المغيرين! الذين يؤذون المسلمين بغير فائدة تعود فالجواب عن ذلك انه اذا كف كت العدوان وترك المسلمين وما لهم من ممالك سكنت الدهماء وحمنت الدماءي وما أحقه بان لا ينهى عن خلق وتأتى مثله ولا يأس ببق ونتى4 فعله وقتغرطاى بالروم وهى بلاد فى ايديكم وخاحها يجى اليكم وقد سفك فيا وقتلة وسبى وهتك واع الاحرار وابى آلا التمادى على الاضرار والاصرار» ومن المشافبة انه اذا حصل التصتم5 على ان لا تبطل هذد الغارات ولا تفتر عن هذه الاثارات فبعّن" مكانا يكون فيه اللقاء وحطى الله النص لمن يشاء فالجواب عن ذلك انّ الاماكن التى افق فيا الملتقى
to keep the integrity of your possessions on land and on sea: that consequently, you feel no need to send troops to conquests, which do all the harm possible to the Muslims, without any utility resulting from it. We will answer you that, if you abstain from all aggression, if you leave the Muslims quiet, and their kingdoms, the peoples will find calm, and the blood will cease to flow in torrents. "How true it is that one must not forbid to another what one does oneself, not command people to act piously, while oneself, one forgets to do it! Khunguradai in Rum, which is a country submitted to your authority, whose taxes are collected in your name, has shed torrents of blood; he has massacred the inhabitants, and he has reduced them to servitude; he has violated the women, and sold the noble ladies as slaves; he has obstinately refused not to continue his violences and his crimes. "Among other things, it has been said to us on your behalf that you have the firm intention not to put an end to these expeditions, and not to
cease these devastations, that consequently you will determine a place where we will meet, and that Allah will give victory to whom it will please Him. We will answer you on this object that there exist (in this world)


[p. 525] {#po.14.p525}
للجمعان! مزة ومزة ومرّةة قد عاف مواردها من سلم من اولائك القوم وخاف ان يعاودها فيعاوده مصرع ذلك اليوم فوقت اللقاء3 علمه عند الله3 فلا يدر وما النصر ألا من عند الله لمن اقدر لا لمن قتر4© ولا نحن معن ينتظر فلتة ولا من له الى غير ذلك لفتة وما امر ساعة النصر آلا كالساعة النى5 لا تأنى آلا بختة الله الموثق لما فيه صلاح هذه الاتمة والقادر على اتمام كل خير ونعمة8© وفيا توثى القاضى شمس الدين ابن خلكان وفيها استّت البدنة بين السلطان وين اهل عكا مدا عشرة سنين7 والحمد لله وحدن»
battlefields, on which encounters have occurred between our two armies, once, twice, three times. The men of your nation who got out of it safe and sound dread these places, they fear returning there, in the terror that the defeat of those days might fall upon them again. As for the day when we will meet, it is Allah who alone has the knowledge of it, and no one could foresee it. Victory does not come from other than Allah, it comes for him in favor of whom He has decided it, it does not come for him who has sought to open the ways to it. "We are not of those who wait for events to come to surprise them, nor, on the contrary, of those men who scrutinize destiny to guess its course. Allah has not made the hour of victory other than this other hour that never rings except unexpectedly, without one having waited for it. Allah favors His people with everything that is for them salvation; He possesses the supreme power to accomplish all good and to grant all grace."
This year, the qadi Shams ad-Din ibn Khallikan died. This same year, the truce was confirmed between the sultan and the population of Saint-Jean d'Acre for a duration of ten years. Praise be rendered to Allah, the Unique!


[p. 526] {#po.14.p526}
' وفى سنة اثنين! وثمانين وستمائة وصل الشيخ عبد الرحمان ومعه جمعا كثيرا رسولا من عند السلطان احمد اغا وكان وصوله الى دمشق ليلة الثاثاء ثانى عشر ذى الحجة فانزلوهم بقلعة دمشق واطلق له فى كل يوم الف درهم نقرةٍ وكان فى صحته مائة وخسين نف وحض فى خدمته اين التيتى4 وزير صاحب ماردين© وكان هذا الشيخ عبد الرحمن له عند السلطان احمد اغا صورة كبيرة وكان يركب فى سائثر بلاد التتار بالجنر على رأسه وسيّر يقول ما ادخل الى بلادكم وامشى آلا بالنبار والجر على رأسى فلما
In the year 682, the shaykh 'Abd ar-Rahman arrived in Damascus, accompanied by a numerous retinue, in the capacity of ambassador, sent by Sultan Ahmad. He arrived in Damascus, during the night of Tuesday, the twelfth day of the month of Dhu al-Hijjah; these people were made to descend in the citadel of Damascus; one assigned each day to 'Abd ar-Rahman a sum of one thousand silver dirhams; he was accompanied by one hundred and fifty persons, and Ibn al-Ta'iti, vizier of the prince of Mardin, was among the people of his retinue. This shaykh 'Abd ar-Rahman enjoyed great credit with Sultan Ahmad: he paraded on horseback, in the whole extent of the empire of the Tatars, having the parasol carried above his head. He sent to say (to the officers of the sultan of Egypt): "I will not enter into your empire, and I will not travel there, on any other condition than to do it during the day, the parasol carried above my head." When he reached the Euphrates, one


[p. 527] {#po.14.p527}
وصل الى الفراةة سروا اليه من حلب جمال الدين اقوش الفارسى فى عسكى من حلب بتاقوانه فلا عتى الفراة! الى ناحيتهم ساروا به فى الليل فاراد الرجوع فلم يمكنود واغلظوا عليه بالقول ولم يمكنوه من رفع الجتر واقام الى سنة ثلاث وثمانين وستمائة وسيأتى ذكرلا ان شاء اللّه تعالى ا
sent to him from Aleppo Jamal ad-Din Ughush al-Farisi, at the head of a detachment of troops from Aleppo, to go to receive him (at his entry onto the lands of Qalawun). When the shaykh 'Abd ar-Rahman had crossed the Euphrates to pass onto the Egyptian lands, (the soldiers of Jamal ad-Din) made him travel during the night; he wanted to return (to the Mongol empire), but they did not leave him the means; they treated him roughly, and did not permit him to have the parasol carried above his head. He remained (in Damascus) until the year 683, and the rest of his history will be told further on, if it pleases Allah, the Most High... In the month of Muharram of the year 688, Sultan al-Malik al-Mansour


[p. 528] {#po.14.p528}
departed from Cairo, leaving in this city, to govern there in his name, his son, al-Malik al-Ashraf Salah ad-Din Khalil; he headed toward Damascus, where he made his entry, the thirteenth day of the month of Safar; he departed from it, the twentieth of this same month, to go to besiege Tripoli. The city surrendered, the fourth day of the month of Rabi' I, after a siege of thirty-four days. In the year 495, Saint-Gilles came to besiege Tripoli; he was called Moembot (= Bohemond), this name meaning "happy", and Saint-Gilles being the name of the city from which this personage was native. Saint-Gilles died before the place in 500, and he was replaced in the command of the Franks by as-Sordani. The Franks seized Tripoli, on Tuesday, the third day of the month of Dhu al-Hijjah 502, after a siege that had lasted seven years and four months; as-Sordani reigned without contest over the city until the moment when a ship from the countries of the West arrived. There was in this ship a
الى ان قدم مركب من بلاد الغرب وفيه صتى من أولاد صنجيل! اسمه بتران ومعه جماعة شيوخ من اصحاب ابيه بخدموه»ه فحضروا عند السردانى3 وقالوا له هذا ولد صتجيل! الملك وهو يريد مدينة والده فقام السردائى4 ورفسه برجله فريالا من على السرير واخرجه فاخذوه اصحاب صنجيل! وطافوا به على الفرنج والفرسان فرخموه وتذكّروا الايمان النى5 لابيه© وقالوا اذا كان فى غد احضرو ونحن حلوس عند السردانى فلما حضروا وخاطبوه فيه قام الفرسان كلهم على السردانى واخرجوا من المملكة وسلموها
a child, who was one of the sons of Saint-Gilles, and who was named Batran. He was accompanied by a certain number of elders who had been his father's officers and who were in his service. These personages went to as-Sordani and said to him: "Here is the son of King Saint-Gilles, who wants his father's city." As-Sordani stood up, kicked him, threw him from the top of the dais upon which the throne was set, and had him put out the door. The officers of Saint-Gilles left with the young prince; they went to find the Franks and the knights, who took pity on him and remembered the oaths they had sworn to his father. They said to them: "Bring the prince tomorrow; we shall be gathered at as-Sordani's." When the officers of Saint-Gilles had presented themselves to as-Sordani, and when they had made a speech to him regarding the young prince, the knights all revolted against as-Sordani; they drove him from the kingdom and gave it to the young man. The latter kept the sovereignty until the day when Barvadj killed him, on Sunday, the fourth day of the month of Rajab in the year 531.


[p. 529] {#po.14.p529}
للصغير فاقام مالكها الى ان قتله برواج فى يوم الاحد رابع رجب سنة احدة وثلئين وخمس مائة© وقتل أكثر اصحابه واستخاف فى طرابلس ! ولده القمص بدران فاسر الاتابك زتكى وضايقهم فصالحه الملك على تسليم حصن بعرين واستخلص القومص صاحب طرابلس وجميع الاسرى وعاد القومص الى طرابلس واقام يها حتّى وئب عليه الاسماعيليّة فقتلوهة فتولى بعده ولده ريمند وهو صتى وحض الحرب مع الفرنج فكسر! الملك العادل نور الدين الشهيد الفرنج على حارم وقتل منهم مقتلة عظيمة وقتل القمص فى الجملة وذلك فى سنة تسع وخسين وخس مائة© فيكون بين ما ملكوها الفرنج وعودها للمسلمين مائة اربعة وعشرين سنةة واربعة شهور واحد عشر. يوما ومن عجيب الاتّفاق ان الفرنج اخذوها من المسلمين يوم
Most of his officers were killed; he left as his successor in Tripoli his son, Count Badran; the atabeg Zengi took him prisoner, then reduced (the Franks) to the last extremity (in Ba'rin). The king (of Jerusalem) made peace with Zengi on the condition of handing over to him the fortress of Ba'rin. The count, lord of Tripoli, recovered his liberty, as did all the prisoners; he returned to Tripoli, where he remained until he was attacked by the Ismailis, who killed him. After him, his son Raymond reigned, who was a young man and who marched to war with the Franks. Al-Malik al-'Adil Nur ad-Din, the Martyr, defeated the Franks at Harim and killed a considerable number of their soldiers; the count was killed with his men-at-arms. This took place in the year 559. The time that elapsed between the era when the Franks seized Tripoli and the moment when this city returned to the possession of the Muslims is one hundred and twenty-four years, four months, and eleven days. As an extraordinary coincidence, it happens that the Franks took this city from the Muslims on a Tuesday, and it was also on a Tuesday that it returned to the power of the followers of Islam. (When it was conquered by the Franks), it obeyed the caliph of Egypt, and it was (when it was taken


[p. 530] {#po.14.p530}


[p. 531] {#po.14.p531}
الثلثا وعوذها الى المسلمين يوم الثلان” وامرها يومئذ الى خليفة مصر وفاتحا الان ملك مصر والحمد والمنّة لله تعالى© قال المؤتّخ ع ان السلطان بعد خراب طرابلس مر بتجديد نمدينة عند حصن ابن صنجيل! وهو على ميل من طرابلس فبنيت المدينة هناك وسكنبا الناس© ولمعا كان السلطان منازل طرابلس قدم عليه رسل صاحب سيس وطلبوا مرضاة السلطان وأنهوا انّ صاحبهم داخل فى حميغ ما يرسم له به فخلع عليهم وعوفيم أنه يسلم مرعش وباهسنا" وقوم بالقطيعة على العادة مع عد اقتراحات أخر وتوجبوانة ثم بعد الفراغ من ام طرابلس عادت الرسل والسلطان نازل على حمص بهدنة جليلة واعتذارات عن تسليم مرعش ومسناه؟ ونه لا يمكنه تسليمهما بسبب إلتثار وبذل للسلطان عوضيما حمل كيرد
from the Franks) the king of Egypt who seized it. Praises and thanks be rendered to Allah, the Most High. The historian said: Then, the sultan, after having had Tripoli razed, ordered a new city to be built near the fortress of the son of Saint-Gilles, which was located a mile from Tripoli. A city was built in this place, and people came to inhabit it. While the sultan was occupied with the siege of Tripoli, ambassadors from the prince of Sis came to find him (they implored his mercy) and did everything possible to obtain his approval; they brought to his knowledge that their master was disposed to hear everything the sultan would order him. The sultan gave them robes of honor; he warned them that the lord of Sis would have to hand over to him the cities of Mar'ash and Bahasna, that he would remain at al-Kati'a according to the previous status, but with a certain number of new conditions. The ambassadors withdrew. Then, when the sultan had overcome the resistance of Tripoli, the ambassadors returned and found the sultan camped at Homs; they brought the draft of a completely satisfactory truce and excuses for not being able to hand over Mar'ash and Bahasna, alleging that it was absolutely
impossible for the king of Armenia to dispose of these two cities because of the Tatars. The prince of Sis, in compensation, paid the sultan a considerable sum of money.


[p. 532] {#po.14.p532}
وفى سنة تسع وثمائين وستماثة للبجرة طلب السلطان سر الاعسس من الم وقال له ائما وآبتك شاد الدواوين بالشأم آلا حتى تنبض فى مصالحى وتحصل الاموال فالتزم انه يحمل فى كل يوم الى بيت المال عشرين الف درهم فظع عليه وسقرد الى الثأم وكتب على يده تتذاكر واضاف اليه شا الشأم بكماله مع بلاد حلب وسائر الحصون وايضا شاد ديوان الجيوش بالشأم» وفيها رسم السلطان لض الدين الافرم بالتوجّه الى دمشق وتجيز المجايق والزردخاناة لاجل حصار عكا فانّه لا وصل الى ديار مصر اقام مدّةّ والاخار ترد عليه ان الفرنج الذين "بعكا قد تكثوا المهادنة وقتلوا فى عكا جماعة من المسلمين من التجاد وغيرهم فعند ذلك تجهّز السلطان وخرج بالجيش جميعه طالب عكا! وذلك فى الثامن عشر
In the year 689 of the Hegira, the sultan had the emir Sonkor al-A'sar come from Damascus and said to him: "I have appointed you inspector of administrative services in Damascus so that you may occupy yourself exclusively and with zeal with my affairs, and so that you may collect the taxes." He imposed upon him the obligation to have a sum of twenty thousand dirhams enter the treasury each day, then he gave him a robe of honor and sent him back to Damascus. The sultan wrote with his own hand the rescripts (investing him with these functions); he added to his charge that of inspector of Syria in its entirety, including the country of Aleppo and all the fortresses; he also appointed him inspector of the military bureaus in Syria. That same year, the sultan sent to 'Izz ad-Din al-Afram a rescript enjoining him to go to Damascus and prepare the ballistae and the parks to go and lay siege to Saint-Jean d'Acre. When the sultan returned (from Syria) to Egypt, he remained in that region for a certain time; he received news there informing him that the Franks who resided in Saint-Jean d'Acre had broken the truce (that he had granted them) and that they had massacred a certain number of Muslims in that city, both merchants and other persons. When the sultan had received this news,
he made his preparations for departure and set out with the entire army to reach Saint-Jean d'Acre, this on the eighteenth day of the month of Shawwal of that year: he came to camp before the mosque of Tibn.


[p. 533] {#po.14.p533}
من شتوال من هذه السنة ونزل بظاهر مسجد التبن !8 ذكر وفاة السلطان الملك المنصور قلاوون قال المؤتّخ3 فلمًا نزل مسيد التبن تشتّش واقام كذلك تسعة عشر يوما الى .يوم السبت سابع ذى القعدة توقّى الى رحمة الله تعالى وكان ولده الملك الاشرف حاضر4 فركب من ساعته وطلع الى القاعة ووقف طرنطاى ورفع الخزانة وركب الجيش جميعه الى ان طلع القلعة» قال المؤتّخ كان مدة مملكته احدى عشر سنة وثلاثة اشهر وومين وذلك لتمام احد وارسين سنة وسّة شهور وعشرين يوماة للدولة. التركتية مع تسلطن ولده
Account of the death of the sultan al-Malik al-Mansour Qalawun. When the sultan came to camp at the mosque of Tibn, he felt very unwell, and he remained in this state for nineteen days, until Saturday the seventh of the month of Dhu al-Qi'dah, when he died. His son, al-Malik al-Ashraf, was present. He mounted his horse at once and went to the Mountain Fortress. (The emir Hosam ad-Din) Dourounadai stopped the expedition, took the treasury, and the entire army got into the saddle to go up to the Fortress. The historian said: The duration of his reign was eleven years, three months, and two days, which made in all forty-one years, six months, and twenty days for the sovereignty of the Turks. After him, his son, al-Malik al-Ashraf, reigned.
الملك الاشرف» الثامن من ملوك الترك السلطان الملك الاشرف صلاح الدين خليل: ابن الملك
The eighth of the kings of the Turks was the sultan al-Malik al-Ashraf Salah ad-Din Khalil, son of al-Malik al-Mansour Qalawun. He ascended the throne on the fifteenth day of the month of Dhu al-Qi'dah of the year 689: he went on horseback, in


[p. 534] {#po.14.p534}
المنصور قلاوون كان جلوسه فى الخامس عش من ذى الفعدة سنة تمع وثمانين وستمائة وركب كجارى عادة الملوك من قلعة الجبل الى الميدان تحت القلعة بعد ان خلع على جميع الامراء والمقتّمين والقضاة والكبار واعيان الدولة بعد ان حدّدوا الايمان له» وطلع من الميدان الى القلعة وض على طرنطاى وعلى كتبغا! فامًا طرنطاى انهه قتله سرييا واحتاط على جميع موحوده فى سائر البلاد وقى فى محسه ثمانية أيّام وهو مّت تع اخرج من القاعة ليلة الجمعة سادس وعشرين ذى القعدة محمول على جنوة الى زاوية الشيخ ابى السعود فغسله الشيخ عمر السعودى3 وكمنه ودفنه بظاهى الزاوية فلعا تسلطن
great pomp, as was the traditional custom of the monarchs, from the Mountain Fortress to the Hippodrome below the Citadel, after having given robes of honor to all the emirs, commanders of the troops, qadis, great personages, and dignitaries of the State, when they had renewed to him the oath of fidelity that had been sworn to him. He went back up from the Hippodrome to the Fortress and had (Hosam ad-Din) Dourounadai and (Zain ad-Din) Ketbuqa arrested. As for Dourounadai, he had him killed on the spot and confiscated everything he possessed throughout the empire. The emir remained eight days in his prison, even though he was dead; he was taken out of the Mountain Fortress during the night of Friday, the twenty-sixth day of the month of Dhu al-Qi'dah, carried on a hurdle to the hermitage of the shaykh Abous-So'oud. The shaykh 'Omar as-So'oudi washed the corpse, wrapped it in a shroud, and buried it outside the hermitage. When Ketbuqa had become sultan, he had the body of Dourounadai transported to
the college that this emir had had built near his hotel in the street of the crossbow makers.


[p. 535] {#po.14.p535}
كيتبفا! نقله الى مدرسته التى بجوار دار« بالبندقانتين© وحكى الامير نجم الدين ابو المعالى أنّ حملة ما اخذ له من داره وحمل الى القلعة وانفق فى الامراة والعساكر من الذهب سثماثة الف دينار مصريّة ومن النقرة ماثئة وسعين قنطار" فضَّة بالقنطار المصرى واخذ السلطان من مماليكه عادة كثيرة وفزق الباقى على الامرآ ومن الخيل والحجورة والجمال
The emir Najm ad-Din Aboul-Ma'ali recounted that the total of what was confiscated from him in his hotel, of what was transported to the Mountain Fortress, and of what was distributed to the emirs and the troops, amounted in gold currency to six hundred thousand Egyptian dinars, and in white coins to one hundred and seventy kintars of silver, in the kintar of Misr. The sultan took for himself a considerable number of Dourounadai's mamluks, and he distributed the rest to the emirs; he also took a number of horses, mares, camels, and dromedaries that it is impossible to evaluate, as well as an immense quantity of copper objects inlaid with silver, silver vases,


[p. 536] {#po.14.p536}
والبجن ما لا يمع عليه حصر ومن النحاس المكتقت! ولاوانى الفضّة و3 صياغة الفرنج وحواصل غلال شيئا كثيرا فسبحان من له الملك©»
pieces of goldsmithery made by the Franks, as well as enormous deposits of grain. Praises be rendered to Him who possesses all power! At the beginning of the year 690, the caliph al-Hakim was reigning, as was his custom; the sultan of Egypt and Syria, from Dongola to the Euphrates, was al-Malik al-Ashraf; the caliph of the Maghreb, in Tunis, was Abu Abd Allah Mohammad ibn Yahya, son of the sultan Mohammad, of whom mention was made above; the sovereign of Yemen was al-Malik al-Muzaffar Shams
ودخات سنة تسعين وسثمائة والخليفة الحاكم بحاله وسلطان مصر والشأم من دنقلة الى الفراةك الملك الاشرى.وخليفة المغرب بتونس ابىة عبد الله متحتد بن يحبى ابن السلطان محتئد المقتم ذكر وصاحب اليمن الملك المظقر شمس الدين يوسف ابن الملك المنصور فى الدين عم بن على ابن رسول وصاحب مكة انجم الدين ابو المي محقد بن ادرس ابن قتّادة الحسنى " والمدينة ع الدين حماز ابن شيحة الحسنى© وصاحب الروم غياث الدين كيضروا9 ابن ركن الدين قليج ارسلان صورة وانما ناب التتار المتحكمين” فى البلاد لا سيّما من حيث ولّى البرواناة فانّه احيا بيت سلجوق وصاحب ماردين الملك
ad-Din Yusuf, son of al-Malik al-Mansour Taqi ad-Din 'Omar, son of Ali, son of Rasul; the prince of Mecca, Najm ad-Din Abu Nama Mohammad ibn Idris ibn Qatada al-Hasani; the prince of Medina, 'Izz ad-Din Jammaz ibn Shiha al-Hasani. The sovereign of the land of Rum was, nominally, Ghiyas ad-Din Kai-Khusraw, son of Rukn ad-Din Kilij Arslan; in fact, it was the Tatar generals who exercised authority in his states, especially since the parvana had departed for the other world, for it was he who had revived the Seljuk dynasty. In Mardin, al-Malik al-Muzaffar Kara Arslan reigned, son of al-Malik as-Sa'id Il-Ghazi the Artuqid; in Hamah, al-Malik al-Muzaffar Taqi ad-Din Mahmoud, son of al-Malik al-Mansour Nasir ad-Din Mohammad, son of al-Malik al-Muzaffar Mahmoud ibn Shahanshah ibn
المظتمر قرارسلان ابن الملك السعيد ايلغازى8 الارتقى وصاحب حماة الملك المظقّر فى الدين: محدرة ابن النتصون تاضى الديق محته إن الظلكي محمود ابن هامتماد ابن اتوب وملك الشرق بمملكة التتار ارغون ابن .ابغاه وفى هذه السنة فى عاشر المحم نقل السلطان الشهيد الملك المنصور قلاوون من قلعة الجل ودخاوا به من باب اللرقيّة وصلوا عليه بجامع الازهر وحمل الى تريته ودفن بالقبّة ونزل مع! القبر بيدرا والشجاعى والحدود© وفيها فى يوم الثلاا العشرين من المحرّم وصل الصاحب شمس الدين ابن السلعوس من الحجاز على البجن الى القاهرةٍ واجتمع بالسلطان© فلا كان اليوم الثالك من
Ayyub; the sovereign of the Orient, in the empire of the Tatars, was Argun, son of Abagha. This year, on the tenth day of the month of Muharram, the body of the martyr sultan al-Malik al-Mansour Qalawun was transferred from the Mountain Fortress; it was taken through the Bab al-Barkiyya gate and the prayer was recited over his coffin in the al-Azhar mosque; it was then carried to the monument he had had built for himself, and he was buried under the dome; Baidara and ash-Shuja'i descended into the grave with the coffin, and it was sealed in its sepulcher. That same year, on Tuesday, the twentieth day of the month of Muharram, the sahib Shams ad-Din ibn as-Sal'ous arrived from the Hijaz in Cairo, mounted on a dromedary, and he had an interview with the sultan. On the third day of his arrival, he received the robes of honor conferring upon him


[p. 537] {#po.14.p537}


[p. 538] {#po.14.p538}
وصوله نزات اليه الخلع بالوزارة وحكم ونفذ الاشغال من يومه وكتب تقليده محى الدين ابن عبد الظاهر بخطهة وركب فى دست ما ركيت الخلفاء فى مثله وفى خدمته الاميى بهاء الدين' بغدى الدوادار الاشرفى والطواشى مرشد ورؤساء مصر والقاهرة وقضائها وحكاممات ” وفها فى سابع عشر صفر قبض السلطان على الامير شمس الدين سنقر الاشقر وعلى سيف الدين حريك ! الناصرى وافرج عن كتبغا وردّ عليه اقطاعه8 وفيها فى سلخ صفر وصل الامير عت الدين الافرم الى دمشق المحروسة لتجبيز المجائيق والزردخانةة لاجل حصار عكا ونودى فى جامع دمشق يوم الجمعة الغزاة الى عكا وشرع الناس من
the office of vizier; he exercised the power of it and dispatched affairs from that very moment. It was Mohyi ad-Din ibn 'Abd az-Zahir who wrote with his own hand the diploma investing him with these functions. Shams ad-Din ibn as-Sal'ous paraded on horseback amidst such pomp as that which once surrounded the (Fatimid) caliphs; the emir Baha ad-Din Boghdi, the dawadar, a mamluk of al-Malik al-Ashraf, walked in the procession that followed him, as did the tawashi Murshid, the important personages of Cairo and Misr, and the qadis and magistrates of these cities. This year, on the seventeenth day of the month of Safar, the sultan had the emirs Shams ad-Din Sonkor al-Ashqar and Saif ad-Din Cherik an-Nasiri arrested, and he had Ketbuqa set at liberty, to whom he returned his fief. This year, on the last day of the month of Safar, the emir 'Izz ad-Din al-Afram arrived in Damascus, the well-guarded, to prepare there the ballistae and the ammunition parks in view of the siege of Saint-Jean d'Acre. On Friday, the expedition intended to retake
the city from the Franks was proclaimed in the great mosque of Damascus. The soldiers began to bring out the ballistae (from the arsenal) and to convey them, from one of the first ten days of the month of


[p. 539] {#po.14.p539}
العشر الازل من ريع الاقك فى خروج المجانيق وجزها الى ثانى عشر ريع الاقل تتجرأت! وسافرت فاليا مع الامير علم الدين الدواارى الى يوم العشرين من الشهر سافر الامير حسام الدين لاحجين نائب الشأم بالجيش الشامى© ووصل الملك المظتّر من حماة بزردخاناةة ورحلة كثيرة وفى يوم الاثنين رابع وعشرين ريع الاقل وصل سيف الدين بلبان الطبّاخى وصحبته عسكر طراباس وحصن الاكراد مع جميع حصون الساحل وصحتهم مجائيق وزردضاناة" ورحالة كثرة"» قال المؤترخ واممًا السلطان الملك الاشرف فانّه عمل فى ليلة الجمعة الثامن والعشرين من صفر يرية والدد مه عظيم7 انفق فيه اموالا
Rabi' I, until the twelfth of that same month, the date on which these pieces were distributed into batteries, and when they departed; the first echelon departed with the emir 'Alam ad-Din ad-Dawadari, and this continued until the twentieth of that month, the date on which the emir Hosam ad-Din Latchin, governor of Damascus, set out with the Syrian army. Al-Malik al-Muzaffar arrived from Hamah with artillery parks and numerous infantry troops. On Monday, the twenty-fourth day of the month of Rabi' I, Saif ad-Din Balaban at-Tabbakhi arrived, bringing with him the troops that held the garrison at Tripoli, in the Citadel of the Kurds, as well as in all the strongholds of the coast of Syria; they brought with them ballistae and parks, as well as numerous infantry. The historian said: As for the sultan al-Malik al-Ashraf, he had celebrated, during the night of Friday, the twenty-eighth day of Safar, in the monument


[p. 540] {#po.14.p540}
عثليمة ونزل السلطان من القلعة لزيارة قبر والده وفق فى الفقراءً والقرّا وعلى جميع اهل المدارس والزوايا والربط جملة كثيرة تقدير خمسة وارسين الف درهم ومن التياب! نحو الف توب *© نمم سافن بالعساكر من الديار المصريّة قاصدا لفتح عكا فى ثالث ذى القعدة ونزل عليه وحاصرها الى ثامن حمادى الال حصل حينئذ للسكر شوش عظيم بسبب حسام الدين لاحين نائب الشأم وعام الدين ابو خرص فانه مسكيم وسيّرك الى دمشق بالحوطة عليهما وعلى توابهماه وكان قبل ذلك قد حاء الامير علم الدين ابو خرص الى
of his father, a great ceremony, for which he spent considerable sums. The sultan descended from the Mountain Fortress to go and pray at his father's tomb, and he distributed to the Sufis, to the readers of the Koran, and to all the religious of the colleges, hermitages, and convents who attended this commemoration, an enormous sum, which amounted to about forty-five thousand dirhams, and about a thousand garments. Then, he left the Egyptian provinces with the army, with the intention of going to seize Saint-Jean d'Acre; on the third day of the month of Dhu al-Qi'dah, he came to camp before that place, and he pursued the siege until the eighth day of Jumada I; on that date, great troubles broke out in the army due to Hosam ad-Din Latchin, governor of Damascus, and 'Alam ad-Din Abu Khirs. (The sultan) had these two generals arrested and sent them to Damascus under good guard and closely watched,
as well as their officers. At an earlier date, Abu Khirs had come to find Hosam ad-Din


[p. 541] {#po.14.p541}
حسام الدين لاجين وقال له السلطان يريد يمسسكك فهّب ثقله واراد البروب وكان نازلا بالقرب من علم الدين الدواقارى فركب وساق خلفه ورده وقال لا تكن سبب هلاك المسلمين فان الفرنج ان علموا بهرويك خرحوا على المسلمين8 مع منه ورجع فلتا كان ثانى يوم احضرا السلطان واخلع عليه وطعنه وطبّب قلبه يومين ثعّ انه فى اليوم الثالث مسكه وسيّرنا الى صفد تحت الحوطة والترسيم ثم سيلا من صفد الى مصره ذكر فتح عكاه كان نزول السلطان عليها يوم الخميس ثالث ريع الاخن واستمى الحصار الى سادس عش حمادى الال ثم عزم السلطان على الزحف فِرب الكوسات على ثاثمائة
Latchin and had said to him: "The sultan wants to have you arrested." Latchin had his baggage leave in great haste and wanted to flee; he was then camped in the vicinity of the emir 'Alam ad-Din al-Dawadari, who mounted his horse, set out on his trail, and implored him to renounce his project, saying to him: "Do not provoke the loss of the Muslims, for if the Franks come to learn of your flight, they will make a sortie against us." Hosam ad-Din Latchin listened to his exhortations and turned back. The next day, the sultan summoned him to his person; he gave him a robe of honor, calmed his anxieties, and reassured him for two days. Then, on the third day, he had him arrested and sent him to Safad under good guard and closely watched; then, he had him taken from Safad to Cairo. Account of the taking of Saint-Jean d'Acre. The sultan came to camp before this place on Thursday, the third day of the month of Rabi' II, and the siege dragged on until the sixteenth of Jumada I. On that date, the sultan decided to have the assault given; he had the kettledrums loaded onto three hundred camels


[p. 542] {#po.14.p542}
حمل ثم زحف عليها .يوم الجمعة قبل طلوع الشمس سابع عشر الشهر ودتت الكوسات وطلعت المسلمين! على الاسوار قال ” المقى الركنى بيبرس الدواقار فى تأريخه الى كنت يومئذ اذذاك بالكرك فلا بلغنى امن هذا الغزاة واجتماع العساكر الاسلامّة اليا من الاقطار والجبات وامى بنقل الزردخانات فقات اليها تاقت نفسى لمباشرتها© فاستأذنت السلطان فى الحضور فامر لى بالدستور فتجتهزت الها من الكرك واستبشرت بنجح الدرك واستصحبت من رحلا النافعين وجماعغة من الححّارين والنجّارين ومن عليت منه الاحتهاد ولنفع فى الحصار والجهاد» وخرحت متوجّها فصادفت اللطان بغزة فرشب بى وتوجّت صحبته فنزلنا الى عكنا واخذ الفرنج فى التأهب والاستعداد والجمع والاحتشاد وتواصات
the assault was given on Friday, before sunrise, the seventeenth day of the month; the kettledrums were beaten (all together) and the Muslims scaled the walls of the city. His Excellency Rukn ad-Din Baibars ad-Dawadar said in his chronicle: I was, at that time, at Karak; when I learned the news of this holy war, when I knew that the armies of Islam were concentrating, coming from all the provinces and all the countries to go and attack this city, when I knew the order that had been given to lead artillery parks before its walls, which had indeed been brought there, I felt penetrated by an ardent desire to take part in this campaign. I requested from the sultan the permission to wage this war: he did me the favor of sending me the order; I then made my preparations to go from Karak to Saint-Jean d'Acre, and I rejoiced at having arrived at the end of my desires; I had myself accompanied by the men of the garrison who could be useful there, by a troop of stonecutters and carpenters, as well as by all the people whose zeal I knew, and the services they could render during a siege and in war. I departed from Karak and I rejoined the sultan at Gaza; he reserved for me an
excellent welcome, and I traveled in his company. We came to camp before Saint-Jean d'Acre; the Franks took their dispositions and prepared for the struggle; they gathered troops and mobilized their forces; at the same


[p. 543] {#po.14.p543}
الهم من جواء البحن التجد والامداد ونصبوا المتجيقات واعدوا الالات وحثنوا الاسوار واجتمع الديوتّة والاسبتار» وكان الوصول اليها والدزول عليها فى الرابع من ريع الاخرا من السنة المذكورة ولمّا نزلناها لم يعنى الفرنج بما شاهدود من الكثرة بل لم تنزل ابوابها مشتوحة م الحصار لم تغلق ليلا ولا نهار وصاروا يخرجون خارج السور وقاتلون» ونب السلطان السساكر المنصورة فى الزرحف ورمتها المجانيق الاسلامّة اياما فلم يؤثر
time, relief and reinforcements reached them by the sea routes. They put their ballistae into battery; they arranged all their machines and reinforced the fortifications of their enclosure; the knights of the Temple and those of the Hospital gathered. It was the fourth day of the month of Rabi' II of the aforementioned year that the Muslim army came to camp before Saint-Jean d'Acre and that it invested the place. When we were installed before the city, the Franks were not in the least worried by the spectacle they were contemplating, nor by the multitude of our soldiers; on the contrary, the gates of Saint-Jean d'Acre remained wide open throughout the duration of the siege, and they were not closed, neither during the night nor during the day. The Franks made sorties beyond the surrounding wall and waged battle.
The sultan arranged his victorious troops for the assault, and the Muslim ballistae beat the city with their fire for several days; this produced no effect, and the Franks were not in the least frightened by those


[p. 544] {#po.14.p544}
اثرا ولا رهبوا بمن رمى سهما ولا حجرا ولم يزل الحال كذلك حتى رمى برجا! من ابرجتها التى بالقرب منها فطلعت اليه لاشاهدد وأتأقله” لعلَى اطلع منه على مكان يكون للحيلة فيه مجال او تغرة تلوح منها فرصة للرجال© فوحدته مفردا عن الور وينه وين المدينة خندقاة آخر وسور والسور الثانى مسلط على البرج بحكم انه مفتوح متا يليه ولا يمكن احد الوقوف فيه فعمدت الى شى كثير من اللبود فعمات منها ستارة كبيرة بمقدار بدنة البرج المكشوفة فاقمتها وسترت من يقف من خلفها سترً حماه من تسلّط السورة عليه ثم تنقدمت بملء مخالى الخيل تراب وردمها فى- الخندق الى يلى هذا البرج
who were throwing arrows at them or who were overwhelming them with quarters of rock. This situation lasted without modification until the moment when they fired on one of the towers that were near the city. I climbed onto this tower to examine it and to look at it closely, with the idea that, perhaps, I could discover from its summit a place in which there would be a passage that would be suitable for favoring the development of a stratagem, or a breach that would allow our soldiers to mount the assault. I noticed that this tower was independent of the surrounding wall, that between it and the city there was another moat, and another wall. This second wall commanded the tower, for the latter was open in the part that faced the wall, and no one, in these conditions, could maintain himself there. I procured a considerable quantity of felt, from which I had a large shield made of the dimension of the exposed front of the tower; I had it set up standing, and I thus covered those who had to stand behind it with a protection that would guarantee them against this circumstance that the (second) wall commanded the tower. Then, I gave the order to fill the horses' nosebags with earth


[p. 545] {#po.14.p545}
فلم يست نهار الخميس الث عشر حمادى! الال آلا وقد صار الخندق طرقا مشاوة فرحفنا على مدينة عكا زحفة ملأت قلوب اهلها رعبا ورحفة© واتبع الله هذا الفتح فنوحا تبشرت اسابها وتفتئحت ابوابها ودلت للعساكر الاسلاميّة صعايات ولما يس الله هذ الفتوح المعجلة وحصّل المسلمون على هذه المآزب المسهّلة عدت مع السلطان الى الديار المصريّة واقمتها الى الان8 وفيها فى يوم الاحد تاسع عش الشهر حاات البشائر بتسليم مدينة صور وهروب الفرنج منها باجمعهم وفى العشرين منه حاءت البشائر بتسليم صيدا وهروب اهلباه وفى الحادى والعشرين منه حرّدٍ السلطان شمس الدين بنّاء المعروف بابن
and to throw them into the moat that was contiguous to this tower, so as to fill it. The day of Thursday, the thirteenth day of the month of Jumada I, was not over before the moat already offered a practicable path. Then, we launched ourselves at the assault of the city with an impetus that filled the heart of its garrison with dread and terror. Allah made this triumph follow other successes that allowed us to pursue the advantages, which made the gates of the city open before us, and which showed the Muslim troops what the difficulty of this conquest had been. When Allah had gratified us with these rapid victories, when the Muslims had thus attained, easily, these advantages of which they had such a pressing need, I returned to Egypt with the sultan, and I stayed there until the present hour. That same year, on Sunday, the nineteenth day of the month, the happy news of the surrender of the city of Tyre and of the flight of all the Franks who resided there was received. On the twentieth of that same month, one learned happily
of the surrender of Saida and the exodus of the Frankish garrison. On the twenty-first of this month, the sultan had Shams ad-Din al-Banna depart,


[p. 546] {#po.14.p546}
المحتدار امير جاندار البدم مديئة صور قال المؤتّخ ونقل عن الشيخ عماد الدين الاصفبانى فى تأريخه ان فى سنة ثمانية عشر وخمس مائة! هبّت ريح حملت رمل الرصافة الى قلعة حبر وفى تلك السنة فتحت الفرنج مدينة صور وكان والها بستى عر الدين
known by the name of Ibn al-Mahaffdar, emir jandar, to dismantle the city of Tyre. The historian said: It has been reported, according to what the shaykh 'Imad ad-Din al-Isfahani says in his chronicle, that in the year 518, a storm arose that transported the sand from ar-Rusafa to the fortress of Ja'bar. That same year, the Franks seized the city of Tyre. The governor of this city was named 'Izz ad-Din al-Banna, vizier of al-Mansour in Egypt, and he fled to Damascus. Then, the conquest of Tyre was


[p. 547] {#po.14.p547}
بناء! وزير المنصور بمصر نهرب الى دمشق ثم كان فتوحبا على يد الملك الاشرف صاحب مصر وهدمبا على .يد شمس الدين بنّاء امير جاندار فالعجب انفاق الاسمين فى أمر صود8 قال والذى+ استشهد من الامرآء على عكا عر الدين ايبك التزى نقيب الجيوش المنصورة وحجمال الدين اقوش العتمى وبدر الدين بيليك5 المسعودى وعلاء الدين كشتغدى الشمسى وشرف الدين قيران السكزى ومن مقتمى الحلقة ارسة وجماعة قليلة من جملة الاك المنصورة وكان مد الاقامة عابها وحسارها ارسة وأربعين يوماة تمع دخل السلطان الى دمشق وصحبته الصاحب شمس الدين ابن السلعوس يوم الثلثاة ثانى
effected by the arms of al-Malik al-Ashraf, sovereign of Egypt, who had it demolished by Shams ad-Din al-Banna, emir jandar. What is extraordinary is the coincidence of these two names in the history of Tyre. The historian said: The emirs who found martyrdom before Saint-Jean d'Acre were 'Izz ad-Din Aibek al-Izzi, inspector of the victorious armies, Jamal ad-Din Oughoush al-Ghatmi, Badr ad-Din Bilik al-Mas'oudi, 'Ala ad-Din Kishtoghdi ash-Shamsi, Sharaf ad-Din Kiran as-Sakizi, four of the commanders of the imperial guard, and a small number of soldiers of the victorious armies. The duration during which the sultan invested this place and besieged it was forty-four days. The sultan then made his entry into Damascus, accompanied by the sahib Shams ad-Din ibn as-Sal'ous, on Tuesday the twelfth day of the month of Jumada II; the very day he arrived in Damascus, the office of viceroy of Syria was entrusted to the emir 'Alam ad-Din Sinjar ash-Shuja'i in replacement of Latchin. The sultan granted him, in addition to the fief that belonged to the viceroyalty


[p. 548] {#po.14.p548}
عش حمادى الاخر وفى يوم دخوله تولى نابة السلطلة بدمشق الامير علم الدين سنج الشجاعى عوضا عن لاحين وزاد السلطان على اقطاع الثيابة قرية خا وهى من خواض قرى الشأم فلم تبرح فى خاض المملكة ورسم له ان يطلق من الخزانة يقلمه مهما أختار من غير. اعتراض© وفيا فى يوم الاحد ثامن عشر حمادى الاخر تولى الاعس شاة الدواوين بالشأم على عادته وذلك انه توصل الى الوزير ابن السلعوس وتزوّج ابْته فاعادة الى مرتبته8 وفبها يوم الارحاة تاسع عشر رجب سافر السلطان من دمشق طالبا للديار المصريّة وكان قبل ذلك قد وصات بطاقة من الشجاعى بفتح بيروت انه فتحبا بالمخادعة وكذلك سلمت عثليث! فى سادس عش شعان وكذلك انطرطون وجبيل وخلى
of Damascus, the village of Khartaba, one of the villages that were the private property of the sultan in Syria, and which had never ceased to be an integral part of the crown domains. At the same time, the sultan signed a rescript by which he prescribed to this emir that the treasury should have to authorize whatever he might deem fit to request, on a simple paper signed by him, without anything being done to create any obstacle for him whatsoever. That same year, on Sunday, the eighteenth day of the month of Jumada II, al-A'sar was invested with the office of inspector of the administrative bureaus in Damascus, which was his usual function. The cause of this was that this officer had won the favors of the vizier Ibn as-Sal'ous, that he had married his daughter, and that the vizier had had him reinstated in his office. This year, on Wednesday, the nineteenth day of the month of Rajab, the sultan departed from Damascus to return to Egypt; immediately before his departure, he received a dispatch from ash-Shuja'i, which announced to him the taking of Beirut, and that he had made himself master of this city by a ruse of war. It was in the same way that 'Athlith was taken, on the sixteenth day of Sha'ban, then Antartous and Jubail, in such a way that the entire coast of Syria was cleared of the Franks.


[p. 549] {#po.14.p549}
الساحل جميعه من الفرنج» وكان دخول السلطان الى القاهرة .يوم. الاثنين تاسع شعبان دخل من باب النص وخرج من باب زولة والمدينة مزيّنة احسن زنة وكان يوما مشهوداة وفيا فى يوم الارمة امن عش شعبان اخرج السلطان بدر الدين بيسرى من الاعتقال وكان له مت تيع سنين معتقل واعاد اليه اقطاعه اذى كان بيده فى زمان الملك المنصور وفها فى يوم الجمعة رابع رمضان اخرج.الامير شمس الدين سنقن الاشقر وحسام الدين لاحجين وركن الدين طقصوا! وشمس الدين سنقى الطوبل من الاعتقال ور اليهم اقطاعهم© وفيا تولّى القضاء القاضى بدر الدين ابن جماعة وفيا توقى سلامش ابن الظاهر
The Sultan's entry into Cairo took place on Monday, the ninth day of the month of Sha'ban; he entered the city through the Gate of Victory and exited through the Gate of Zawila. The city was decorated in the most splendid fashion, and it was a day of great celebration. That same year, on Thursday, the eighteenth day of the month of Sha'ban, the Sultan released from prison the Emir Badr ad-Din Baïsari, who had been detained for nine years, and he restored to him the fief that had been in his possession during the time of al-Malik al-Mansour (Qalawun). On Friday, the fourth day of the month of Ramadan, the Sultan released from their prison the Emirs Shams ad-Din Sonqor al-Ashqar, Hosam ad-Din Latchin, Rokn ad-Din Toghosou, and Shams ad-Din Sonqor at-Tawil, and he restored their fiefs to them. That same year, the Qadi Badr ad-Din (Muhammad) ibn Jama'a was invested with the functions of Supreme Qadi, and Sulamish, son of al-Malik az-Zahir, died in the city of Constantinople.


[p. 550] {#po.14.p550}
بمدينة اسطنبول© وفيبا هلك ارغون ملك التتار سقى وإنهموا المغل به اليهبود وقبضوا على سعدا الدولة وزير والمستولى على سلطائه ولمعا مات ارغون وأنهموا به اليبود مال المفل على سائر الييود فقتلوهم عن آخرهم ونبوا جميع اموليم وكانت اموالا عظيمة» واختلف كلمة التتار على الملك فمالت طائفة الى بيدوا ولم يوافقوا على كيختوا3 نع وصل كبختوا4 الى الروم وجلس على التخت ثلاة ابّام» وكان قد وصل الخ الى السلطان بذلك وهو فى حصار عكا وكان ارغون. قد عظم شأنه عند المغل منذ قتل عه احمد اغا وكان شجاعا بطلا مقداما حسن الصورة سكا للدماء" شديد البطش والكفر فأراح الله من شر وكفر» ١
This year, Arghun, King of the Tatars, died; he died poisoned, and the Mongols accused the Jews of having committed this crime. They seized the person of Sa'd ad-Daula, his vizier, who directed the affairs of his empire. When Arghun was dead, and the Mongols accused the Jews of this catastrophe, they rushed upon all the Jews, massacred them to the last one, and plundered all their wealth, which amounted to enormous sums. The Tatars were of divergent opinions (regarding the choice of his successor) in the sovereignty; one party felt an inclination for Baidu and did not want Geikhatu.
